2025-06-17T18:39:22.5958528Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5958698Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5959165Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5959854Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5960061Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5960220Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5961923Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5963402Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5963565Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5964185Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5965003Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5965524Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5966074Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5966623Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5967166Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5967761Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5967998Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5969248Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5970608Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5970915Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5971139Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5971326Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5971973Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5972522Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5973304Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5974902Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5975993Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5976493Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5976661Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5977134Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5977321Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5977422Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5977633Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5978144Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5978733Z ```
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5978840Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5978928Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5979153Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5979801Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5980554Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5981018Z ```
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5981183Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5981342Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5981585Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5981686Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5982274Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5982796Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5982870Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5983121Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5983619Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5984131Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5984616Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5985194Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5985505Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5985882Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5986355Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5986434Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5986544Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5986547Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.5986627Z </pr_description>

2025-06-17T18:39:22.6056131Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6056724Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6056848Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6057052Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6057371Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6057990Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6058783Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6059371Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6060036Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6060393Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6061114Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6061580Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6061739Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6062034Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6062397Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6062798Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6063007Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6063111Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6064304Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6065337Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6065415Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6066221Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6067005Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6067498Z Let me know once you've completed the OAuth client setup and added the GitHub secrets, and I'll activate the Tailscale integration.

2025-06-17T18:39:22.6073000Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6073331Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6073729Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6073886Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6074047Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6074273Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6074657Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6074770Z ```json
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6074942Z {
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6075111Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6075345Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6075703Z   },
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6075873Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6076043Z     {
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6076288Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6076551Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6076771Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6076977Z     }
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077228Z   ]
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077390Z }
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077549Z ```
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077641Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077722Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6077940Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6078247Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6078990Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6079640Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6080250Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6080475Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6080578Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6080921Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6081428Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6082097Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6082319Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6082902Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6083440Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6083741Z 
2025-06-17T18:39:22.6084390Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
